# Pinned HTTP route inventory

Baseline: SillyTavern 1.19.0 at `06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127`. Generated from `src/server-main.js`, `src/server-startup.js`, `src/users.js`, and `src/endpoints/**`.

This inventory records route declarations and router mounts from the pinned source. A `partial` route has only the explicitly listed observer coverage; it does not establish middleware or end-to-end parity.

Route registrations found: **339**. STATUS CONVENTION, stated because the distinction is the difference between a decision and a backlog item: a bare `not implemented` means the route is NOT YET DONE and carries no claim beyond that; `not implemented: <reason>` means there is a REASON the route cannot or should not be built as a differential, and the reason names it -- an architecture boundary by number, a package-owned protocol, or an inherited property. A reader should never have to open a second document to tell which kind of `not implemented` they are looking at. Method/path values that depend on computed expressions remain visible in the table for manual source tracing.

| Method | Full path | Upstream declaration | Current status |
|---|---|---|---|
| GET | `/` | [src/server-main.js:214](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/server-main.js#L214) | partial: fresh-start static body/status/content type match the pinned oracle; shared middleware remains unimplemented |
| POST | `/api/anthropic/caption-image` | [src/endpoints/anthropic.js:8](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/anthropic.js#L8) | partial: `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-anthropic-caption.json` (28 scenarios) recorded from the pinned server with its node-fetch replaced by a controlled peer, PLUS two scenarios on a second, UNHOOKED pinned boot pointed at a real local listener, because the stub records the header object the route passed rather than the wire and cannot refuse a connection. Compared per scenario: status, content-type, content-length, ETag, body bytes, outbound method, target, body and header set; each scenario carries a `comparison` block naming what is compared and what is `shape` with a reason. Pinned behaviours preserved: the CATCH branch (a `content[0].text` access that throws -- absent `content`, `[]`, a non-JSON 2xx body, a JSON array -- answers 500 `Internal server error`) is DISTINCT from the FALSY branch (`''`/`0`/`false`/`null` answer 500 `No caption found`); `content` is indexed with JavaScript semantics, so a STRING content takes the falsy branch, `content:{"0":{...}}` answers that member, and `content:[5]` takes the falsy branch; the data URI is split twice with no guard and an `undefined` half is OMITTED by `JSON.stringify`; `reverse_proxy` is string-concatenated, so a trailing slash yields `/v1//messages`; a non-2xx upstream is flattened to the upstream STATUS with `{error:true}` for a JSON or a text body alike; `proxy_password` REPLACES the stored key and, when absent with `reverse_proxy` set, the WIRE carries the literal `undefined` (measured, not reasoned); an absent secret sends an EMPTY `x-api-key` (header present, not dropped); a rejected fetch answers `Internal server error`, NOT `{error:true}`; and a truthy non-string caption is forwarded as a VALUE (`{"caption":42}`). The malformed-body scenario claims only its 400 STATUS because pinned answers express.json's development error page, whose bytes are host-derived. Gaps: no timeout branch (pinned sets none, and the Go side mirrors that, so a hung provider pins a goroutine exactly as upstream); no browser-driven capture, so there is no B/E surface for this route. |
| POST | `/api/assets/character` | [src/endpoints/assets.js:315](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/assets.js#L315) | partial: steps `assets.character.ok`, `.live2d`, `.bgm`, `.noName`, `.badCategory`, `.missingFolder` compare status, content type, content-disposition, the normalised body and its canonical SHA-256, the canonical byte length, and the characters tree; the sanitized name, category gate, `/`-templated non-live2d paths, platform-separator `path.join` for live2d and the empty array for a missing folder are reproduced. CONTENT-LENGTH IS DERIVED FROM THE BODY: the live2d branch's platform separators are JSON-escaped on Windows, so the raw header is HOST-DEPENDENT and is classified `shape` WITH the measured reason (recorded raw 53 vs canonical 49, exactly the four escaped-separator bytes); `normalizedContentLength` (49) is compared instead, and every other JSON branch keeps its raw content-length compared because there it equals the canonical length. Mutations killed: removing the live2d branch; recording the old raw-header form (the fixture-integrity guard now catches that on the RECORDING host, not only on Linux CI). |

| POST | `/api/assets/download` | [src/endpoints/assets.js:190](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/assets.js#L190) | partial: `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-assets-download.json` (13 ordered steps, recorded by `tools/probe-assets-download.mjs`) replayed by `internal/server/assets_download_test.go` compares status, content type, content-disposition, exact body bytes and the whole user tree (path, kind, size, SHA-256) after every step, plus an outbound COUNT per step. The route's point is that it has FOUR guards (`isValidUrl`, the `whitelistImportDomains` exact-match host check, the category check and `validateAssetFileName`) and that its single outbound request happens after all of them, so every refusal step records ZERO outbound requests and every forwarding step exactly one. Reproduced pinned details: the host check is an EXACT whitelist membership (unlike content import's substring provider classification), a refused filename still runs `ensureFoldersExist` first, `res.sendStatus` bodies are status phrases while the filename guard is a text/html message, `node-fetch`'s null-body statuses (101/204/205/304) turn a 2xx into the route's 500, the character category answers the bytes without writing an asset file, and `assets/temp` is created and left behind as a DIRECTORY while its file is unlinked. NOT COVERED: the transient partial temp file a mid-stream failure leaves on pinned (GoTavern writes atomically, so the recorded END states are identical and only the transient artefact differs — a fixture `knownDifferences` entry), the 101/204/205/304 branch (no fixture can express a null body, so it is a labelled derivation test, `TestAssetsDownloadTreatsANullBodyStatusAsAFailure`), and the `mime.lookup` table is a subset of the mime-types database with `application/octet-stream` as the fallback. |

| POST | `/api/backends/chat-completions/generate` | [src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js:2242](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js#L2242) | partial: FIVE real sources close the provider-wire differential against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-provider-wire.json` (39 scenarios): OPENAI (reverse_proxy + proxy_password), CUSTOM (custom_url, CUSTOM secret, YAML include/exclude), OPENROUTER, CLAUDE and MISTRALAI. Each was recorded, not inferred, and the recordings corrected reasoning in three of the five. OPENROUTER is not "openai with a different URL": no reverse_proxy, no custom_url, OPENROUTER_HEADERS (HTTP-Referer, X-Title), and a shaping layer (`transforms` from a switch where `auto`/absent OMITS the member, `plugins` always present, `reasoning:{exclude}` with `effort` added to the SAME object, `provider:{allow_fallbacks,order}`, `quantizations` merged into it, `route:fallback`, `verbosity`, its own `logprobs>0` rule); a non-2xx answers 200 `{error:{message},quota_error}`. CLAUDE builds its own body in a fixed member order with `x-api-key` (never Bearer), `anthropic-version` and an `anthropic-beta` list that is non-empty BY DEFAULT, converts the prompt itself, and sends `thinking{enabled,budget_tokens:1024}` with max_tokens raised for an ABSENT `reasoning_effort` (its budget calculator has no default arm); a non-2xx answers 500 `{error:true}`, and the stream forwards Anthropic frames terminating in `message_stop`, never `[DONE]`. MISTRALAI drops `seed === -1`, sends `stop` only when non-empty, replaces tool ids with `sha512(id)[0:9]`, folds a user turn after a tool turn, forwards tools RAW, maps `json_schema` to `response_format` with `strict ?? true`, and has THREE error observables (400 before the try for a missing key, 500 carrying the PARSED provider body, and **200** for a thrown fetch -- the trap a CLAUDE-shaped implementation falls into). Every source keeps a non-stream CONTROL carrying the same answer as its stream, and a test requires the deltas to spell exactly that content plus the source own terminator. Seams (`GOTAVERN_{OPENROUTER,CLAUDE,MISTRAL}_CHAT_URL`) default to the pinned literals and every captured target is checked against them. MISTRALAI is ALSO browser-measured on E: `tests/browser/generation-mistral-comparison.json` records both legs sending the SAME provider request (7421 B) with the same milestones and all four send-window guards true, both answering 200 with the scripted completion, and `proxyPasswordPresent` true on both legs as a BOOLEAN (never the credential) -- a check the verifier enforces, because with `reverse_proxy` set the pinned server reads `request.body.proxy_password` rather than the source secret and answers a bare 400 `{error:true}` when it is empty. OPENROUTER is ALSO browser-measured on E and C: `tests/browser/generation-openrouter-comparison.json` (both legs send byte-identical provider requests) and `tests/browser/streaming-openrouter-comparison.json` (`compatibleReady: true`; cancellation proved at the provider by `clientDisconnected` on BOTH legs; the streaming assertion is the content-prefix/convergence property, with tick alignment RECORDED and not asserted because the render boundary was measured moving on one leg across two runs of one build, and the legs driven at `PROBE_FRAME_COUNT=14` while the custom artifact describes a different drive). Mutations killed: two for OPENROUTER, four for CLAUDE, four for MISTRALAI. NOT covered, stated: OpenRouter media embedding / reasoning signatures / config-gated prompt caches / the gemini `/models` cache probe; Claude caching helpers and `promptPlaceholder`; Mistral `mistral.enablePrefix`; plus labelled derivations for each source pre-fetch and transport-failure branches. Every OTHER source still answers 501 with ZERO outbound requests -- and for COMETAPI that matches the pinned product rather than describing a gap: pinned assembles the CometAPI URL and key and then unconditionally throws `This provider is temporarily disabled.`, so the pinned server never sends a request for that source. It is the one row where the gap is UPSTREAM's, not GoTavern's. |

All four auth branches are pinned separately: AI Studio (key header, and NO key guard at all: with no secret the request is still sent and the key header goes out EMPTY), Vertex express (three URL shapes including the project-id form and the region-less global host), Vertex full (a token exchange then the model call, `Authorization: Bearer`), and reverse proxy (where the AI Studio branch puts the password in `x-goog-api-key` with NO Authorization header while the Vertex branch uses a bearer token). The no-caching property is pinned by request count: each call performs its own token exchange, so a cached implementation loses the second exchange and fails. Audio answers are compared by digest and length, never as text, because a UTF-8 round trip would corrupt the RIFF header; `audio/l16` becomes a 44-byte RIFF/WAVE mono 16-bit header with the sample rate from `rate=(\d+)` defaulting to 24000, and any other mime type passes through with its own content type. The refusal branches (missing and invalid service-account JSON) answer 500 with ZERO outbound requests. `model` is interpolated RAW into the path and a reserved-character model scenario pins it. It creates a long-running job and then POLLS up to thirty times with `delay(5000 + attempt * 1000)`. The POLL COUNT is a fixture property (timed rules) and is asserted, because it is the only thing that discriminates a cache-like shortcut from the real loop; the recorded scenarios stop at one and two polls so the probe stays fast, and the timeout branch needs thirty polls (about ten minutes of wall clock) so it is a LABELLED Go IMPLEMENTATION test rather than a parity scenario, and the probe deliberately does NOT shorten the delay because that would record a behaviour the product does not have. Two poll shapes are pinned: Vertex POSTs `:fetchPredictOperation` with `{operationName}` and reads `response.videos[0].bytesBase64Encoded`; AI Studio GETs `urlJoin(baseUrl, jobName)`, reads `response.generateVideoResponse.generatedSamples[0].video.uri`, and then FETCHES that provider-supplied URI and base64-encodes the bytes. That URI needs NO production seam: the pinned route reaches it through the same `node-fetch` module every other call uses, so the controlled peer captures it, and the test rewrites the recorded payload to the peer origin and compares the PATH. The contrast is recorded because it is the reason a seam would otherwise have been needed: `zai` refuses any hostname outside `.zai`/`.ufileos.com`, which is a POLICY in the pinned source rather than an unreachable transport. Eight failures each answer 500 with their own TEXT message and are asserted individually (`Video generation request failed`, `No video job name found in response`, `Video job status request failed`, `No video data found in response`, `No video URI found in response`, `Video fetch request failed`, `Video generation timed out`, `Video generation aborted by client`). Cancellation follows the Horde precedent: the parity leg could not be observed, so it is a LABELLED implementation test asserting the abort answer from source, and it covers BOTH abort sites, which is why a mutation that changes only the first one now dies. Pinned parameter details: `personGeneration` is HARDCODED to allow_all here, unlike generate-image which reads gemini.image.personGeneration from config, so UNIFYING THE TWO WOULD BE A COMPATIBILITY BREAK; `durationSeconds` applies the OR operator to an absent member (default 6) and then clamps to the model family bounds, so the bounds depend on the model family; the `seed` member exists ONLY for a veo-3 model and is randomized when absent; `negativePrompt` defaults to the EMPTY STRING rather than being omitted; and the model is interpolated RAW into the path. Elapsed time is recorded as a BUCKET, not a duration, because a millisecond value would be host-derived. Known gaps: the timeout and cancellation branches are implementation tests, not parity, for the reasons above |

Pinnability is NOT the objection: the package calls the global `fetch`, so a controlled peer captures the URL and the form body and a synthetic response is replayable. The objection is OWNERSHIP: the protocol belongs to the dependency rather than to the pinned server, so porting it would make GoTavern own a copy of an undocumented private Google endpoint and track that package forever, to buy one route. It stays not implemented under the dependency-ownership boundary in AGENTS.md, alongside translate/google and translate/bing, and the decision is REVERSIBLE: a user requirement turns it into an explicit product decision with a stated cost rather than a rediscovery |

The groq leading-system-to-user rewrite is asserted with the config PRESENT. ROLE BRANCHES ENUMERATED FROM THE SOURCE, not sampled: exactly two lines touch roles (the config-driven unshift and the groq rewrite), both have scenarios, and a third search hit is a content-part lookup for zai video inlining rather than a role change. api_type: a table of FOURTEEN keys with a count-and-equality assertion. TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY THE REPLAY AND FIXED: reverse_proxy replaces the branch key with proxy_password, and the replay harness had to anchor mocks on EscapedPath because URL.Path is DECODED and turned the workers_ai account id into a different path. INHERITED PROPERTY, reproduced rather than fixed: a no-key branch sends the literal Authorization header Bearer with an EMPTY credential, because the pinned header object is built unconditionally; the handler had ADDED a conditional the pinned code does not have, which is hardening by accident and would change what an upstream provider receives. CAPABILITY SEQUENCE, recorded because the next config-driven route will hit it: a file-write config capability was designed, verified additive (byte-clean, host-side only, two probes re-recording byte-identically), and then RETIRED by a measurement, because the pinned server parses config.yaml at STARTUP and a running process never re-reads it, so the assertion failed at one message instead of two; the replacement is a BOOT-TIME override applied where the real startup writes the file, which REMOVES the shared mutable state rather than managing it and therefore retired the restore, the isolation assertion and the ordering constraint. An additive capability can still be the WRONG capability. P-surface limit: only the declared headers are compared. WHAT THIS partial CLAIM DOES NOT COVER: thirteen of the fourteen api_type getters are TABLE-PRESENT BUT HEADER-UNMEASURED (only openrouter header set and secret were read, so the count assertion pins the KEY SET rather than fourteen working getters); the getOverrideHeaders host override is CONFIG-dependent via getConfigValue(requestOverrides) and is asserted by nothing; and the three user-controlled merges are NOT yet scenario-ed (the three user-controlled behaviours are CUSTOM-BRANCH ONLY, which is why an openai-branch scenario measured nothing for either an object or a string input; mergeObjectWithYaml parses a YAML STRING (a JSON string parses as YAML) and Object.assigns it, so custom_include_body DOES override model and messages because bodyParams spreads after them, and custom_include_headers DOES replace the credential because headers spread after Authorization; custom_exclude_body deletes under the same branch guard. All three are now ASSERTED on the custom branch with server_url set: the includes take a YAML string and OVERRIDE with both sides named (body over model and messages, headers over Authorization), and the exclude deletes in three accepted shapes with two scenarios each deleting a DIFFERENT key so one form cannot stand in for another. The handler is gated on custom and parses the string, doing nothing silently on a failed parse to match the helper catch. What remains unasserted on this route is exactly two MEASUREMENT gaps: the thirteen header-unmeasured api_type getters and the config-dependent host override, neither of which the fixture can currently reach) |

| POST | `/api/openai/chutes/transcribe-audio` | [src/endpoints/openai.js:880](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/openai.js#L880) | partial: implemented and replayed against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-openai-chutes-transcribe.json` (11 scenarios), recorded from the real pinned listener. This is the ONE route in the project where the REQUEST changes the DESTINATION: the pinned handler builds `https://${request.body.model}.chutes.ai/transcribe`, so a model containing a dot or a slash sends the request to a DIFFERENT HOST. That is reproduced VERBATIM with no sanitising and recorded as an INHERITED property, the way the content-import substring match is recorded; hardening it would be a deliberate compatibility change rather than a silent improvement. Two assertions cover the escape risk that follows: the CAPTURED URL is compared with its host visible (a dot model yields `https://evil.example.com/x.chutes.ai/transcribe`, a slash model yields `https://a/b.chutes.ai/transcribe`), and the CAPTURED REQUEST COUNT is asserted, so a request that escaped the controlled peer leaves ZERO captures and fails rather than passing as nothing to compare. OBSERVATION SURFACE, stated precisely because it is weaker than the rest of the fixture: the peer cannot own `evil.example.com`, so the hostile-host behaviour is asserted by a URL-BUILDER test at the seam DEFAULT, NOT by a peer-observed request; the peer-observed surface covers the non-hostile hosts, the body, the headers and the count. That builder test names the hostile case explicitly, so deleting the scenario cannot silently drop it. The seam is the host TEMPLATE (`https://{model}.chutes.ai`) with the pinned path appended, so an override cannot move the path. The body is JSON carrying the uploaded file as base64 (`audio_b64`). The chunk join takes each chunk text with the OR operator, concatenates them and then trims the WHOLE string, and the fixture DISCRIMINATES both halves of that: a scenario with leading and trailing whitespace records `padded  \n second` where the untrimmed value would be `  padded  \n second \n`, and a scenario with INTERNAL whitespace records `a    b` where a per-chunk trim would give `ab` and the untrimmed value would be `  a    b  `. Both scenarios are named in the Go test, so removing either cannot silently re-open the gap. A JSON `null` answer is the only input that reaches the array check (a JSON object fails the earlier unmarshal), and it answers the PLAIN 500 status text rather than the provider body. A provider error forwards the provider TEXT with a 500, and the refusals (no key, no uploaded file) answer 400 with ZERO outbound requests. P-surface limit: only the declared headers are compared (authorization, content-type, host, content-length). Known gaps: the hostile-host case is a builder assertion rather than a peer observation, as stated above |

NO seam beyond the base URL is needed because the host is FIXED, which is a different sentence from the Google video route where the module replacement was required because the URI came from the provider. The POLL COUNT is fixture data and is asserted (three requests for one poll, four for two, and the declared counts in the fixture), because when the requests are otherwise identical the count is the only thing that separates the real loop from a shortcut. The create body reproduces the pinned OR operator: a falsy member falls back to its default, and a TRUTHY NON-STRING member is forwarded AS ITS OWN TYPE, so an empty-array model reaches the provider as an empty array and a numeric duration as a number; the discriminating scenario exists because a string-only helper survived the first mutation run. An absent prompt member is omitted rather than sent as the string undefined. The provider-supplied job id is interpolated RAW into the poll and content paths. Five failure answers are asserted individually: No video job ID returned, the provider TEXT for each of the three failure paths, Video generation failed for a failed status, and Video generation aborted by client. INHERITED PROPERTY, and the reason this needed an explicit ruling: an EXHAUSTED video job HANGS. After thirty attempts the pinned handler falls off the end of its try and answers NOTHING, so the request stays open until the client gives up; the replacement reproduces that deliberately (it blocks on the caller context and writes nothing) because answering would change every client behaviour and a bare return would be 200 with an empty body, which a naive client parses as a video. A client must impose its own timeout. The TIMEOUT and CANCELLATION legs are LABELLED Go IMPLEMENTATION tests and parity was UNOBSERVED for both, the timeout because thirty polls with escalating delays is about ten minutes of wall clock and because the pinned answer is a hang rather than a response; the timeout test asserts thirty attempts, the exact delay sequence, ZERO bytes written and a return once the caller is cancelled. The delay is a package-level seam so the test drives thirty attempts instantly, and the replay overrides it too because the delays affect WHEN the polls happen, not WHAT is recorded. The caller-disconnect decision is explicit at the loop entry, after the sleep and in both transport-error paths, and the outbound work uses its own context, because the pinned fetches are not signal-bound; the Google route taught that a decision depending on whether a cancellation happened to be observed is host-dependent. The replay peer reproduces the STUB matching semantics (first rule whose match is contained in the URL, honouring times), because a consumer that serves mocks by request order hands the poll the content mock. P-surface limit: only the declared headers are compared (authorization, content-type, host, content-length). Known gaps: the timeout and cancellation legs are implementation tests rather than parity, as stated |

| POST | `/api/openai/groq/transcribe-audio` | [src/endpoints/openai.js:862](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/openai.js#L862) | partial: implemented and replayed against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-openai-transcribe.json` (12 scenarios), recorded from the real pinned listener. All four routes share ONE pinned handler (`createTranscribeHandler`), so the Go side is one implementation with four BASE seams whose defaults are asserted (`GOTAVERN_OPENAI_TRANSCRIBE_URL`, `GOTAVERN_GROQ_TRANSCRIBE_URL`, `GOTAVERN_MISTRAL_TRANSCRIBE_URL`, `GOTAVERN_ZAI_TRANSCRIBE_URL`) and the pinned path is APPENDED, so an override cannot silently move it. The body is MULTIPART built from the uploaded file exactly as the pinned form-data FormData does: a `file` part named audio.wav typed audio/wav carrying the uploaded bytes, then `model`, then `language` only when truthy. That is the stream-backed form-data shape whose mis-routing once broke the speech probe, so every multipart scenario asserts the recorded part list is NON-EMPTY and the replay refuses otherwise: a request the peer failed to record can never look like a request the server never made. Parts are compared by name order, filename, content type, digest and length, because the boundary is random per request. `model` is a form FIELD here and is never interpolated into a path, so this family has no rule-15 case; an ABSENT model is reproduced as the string undefined, which is what `formData.append(model, undefined)` writes. The refusals answer 400 with ZERO outbound requests (no key, no uploaded file), asserted as a count per rule 17. A provider error forwards the provider TEXT with a 500 and a success passes the provider JSON through unchanged. P-surface limit: only the declared headers are compared (authorization, content-type, host, content-length); transport-added headers are not asserted. Known gaps: none for these four routes |

Each provider has its OWN seam defaulting to the pinned base and asserted: `GOTAVERN_SD_HUGGINGFACE_URL`, `GOTAVERN_SD_XAI_URL`, `GOTAVERN_SD_CHUTES_IMAGE_URL` and `GOTAVERN_SD_NANOGPT_GENERATE_URL` are new, while electronhub and aimlapi reuse `GOTAVERN_SD_ELECTRONHUB_URL` and `GOTAVERN_SD_AIMLAPI_URL` because their generate call shares that host with their list call. Pinned behaviours preserved: huggingface interpolates the model RAW into the path; electronhub appends only a truthy size and quality; chutes falls back with the OR operator rather than the nullish one; nanogpt and aimlapi forward the WHOLE submitted body re-serialized; xai derives the format from a data URL when the answer is one; aimlapi sends HTTP-Referer and X-Title, accepts images[0] or data[0], accepts b64_json or base64, falls back to fetching the url, and forwards the provider TEXT with a 500. Known gaps: the image bytes are synthetic peer answers, so live provider content is shape only; the URL-fallback host is asserted by path only because it comes from the provider answer |

Pinned behaviours preserved: huggingface interpolates the model RAW into the path; electronhub appends only a truthy size and quality; chutes falls back with the OR operator rather than the nullish one; nanogpt and aimlapi forward the WHOLE submitted body re-serialized; xai derives the format from a data URL when the answer is one; aimlapi sends HTTP-Referer and X-Title, accepts images[0] or data[0], accepts b64_json or base64, falls back to fetching the url, and forwards the provider TEXT with a 500. Known gaps: the image bytes are synthetic peer answers, so live provider content is shape only; the URL-fallback host is asserted by path only because it comes from the provider answer |

THE STALE REASON IS CORRECTED HERE TOO: this route was previously listed `not implemented` because its native FormData parts were said to be unobservable; that claim was WRONG - `tools/lib/pinned-outbound.mjs` `recordBody` now captures `[object FormData]` entries and the timed-rule support exists, so the reason is DELETED and the parts are fixture data. The fixture asserts the part list is NON-EMPTY and the replay parses the multipart body Go actually sent and compares parts by name and value in order, refusing a multipart content type that yields zero parts. One declared exception, in the test comment: on a native FormData request `content-type` is CLIENT-generated framing (the boundary) that the hook cannot record, so the bidirectional header check exempts it and the PARTS are the assertion, not the framing header. HONESTY NOTE: the pinned `undefined`-skip branch is UNREACHABLE from an HTTP JSON body (JSON has no undefined), so no scenario exercises it and the probe assertion no longer claims coverage. Mutations killed with the exact test name and `=== RUN` visible: missing key not 400; unknown model not 500; object part coerced to empty; non-ok treated as success; bearer dropped. |

| POST | `/api/sd/workersai/generate` | [src/endpoints/stable-diffusion.js:2108](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/stable-diffusion.js#L2108) | partial: 12 scenarios (`workersaiNoKey`, `.noAccount`, `.noModel`, `.jsonProjection`, `.jsonTopLevelImage`, `.jsonNoImage`, `.flux2Multipart`, `.binaryAnswer`, `.nonOkForwardsText`, `.reservedModelRaw`, `.falsyMembersOmitted` and the reserved-account case in `.jsonProjection`) replayed by `internal/server/sd_generate_test.go`. Compared: response status, headers and body bytes, and the single outbound request (method, target, declared headers, JSON body or multipart PARTS). Reproduced: the key, the trimmed account id and the trimmed model are each required with ZERO outbound on 400; the account id is `encodeURIComponent`-ed into the path while the MODEL is interpolated RAW (rule 15, exercised by `org/model v1?x=1`); the body projection applies JS truthiness gates and `Number()` conversion (width "512" -> 512, guidance from `scale`) with `seed >= 0` where `null >= 0` is TRUE and a negative seed is omitted; `/flux-2/` selects native FormData instead of JSON; a JSON answer is projected from `result.image` or a top-level `image` and answers 500 when neither exists; a non-JSON answer becomes base64 bytes with `{format:"png", image}`; a non-ok answer forwards the provider TEXT at 500 while a transport error is a plain 500. THE STALE REASON IS CORRECTED HERE TOO: the route was previously listed `not implemented` because its flux-2 branch was said to send native FormData whose parts were unobservable; that claim was WRONG - `recordBody` now captures `[object FormData]` entries and the timed-rule support exists, so the reason is DELETED and the parts are fixture data. The multipart scenario asserts a NON-EMPTY part list and the replay compares parts by name and value in order; the native-FormData `content-type` is exempted from the header check as CLIENT-generated framing, with the parts as the assertion. Mutations killed: account id not encoded; model encoded (the rule-15 case); seed gate removed; flux-2 multipart branch removed; non-ok generic text. |

The chutes generate route talks to image.chutes.ai while its list route talks to api.chutes.ai, and nanogpt generate appends /api/generate-image where its credits route uses /api, so those two get separate seams instead of sharing one. Compared per scenario: status, every header, response BYTES, and the outbound method, encoded path, body, query order and the declared header set in BOTH directions (authorization, x-api-key, accept, http-referer, x-title, content-type). Pinned behaviours preserved: huggingface interpolates the model RAW into the path; electronhub appends only a truthy size and quality; chutes falls back with the OR operator rather than the nullish one; nanogpt and aimlapi forward the WHOLE submitted body re-serialized; xai derives the format from a data URL when the answer is one; aimlapi sends HTTP-Referer and X-Title, accepts images[0] or data[0], accepts b64_json or base64, falls back to fetching the url, and forwards the provider TEXT with a 500. Known gaps: the image bytes are synthetic peer answers, so live provider content is shape only; the URL-fallback host is asserted by path only because it comes from the provider answer |

| POST | `/api/secrets/delete` | [src/endpoints/secrets.js:588](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/secrets.js#L588) | partial: all eight `/api/secrets/*` routes are implemented and replayed against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-secrets.json`, recorded by `tools/probe-secrets.mjs` through the real pinned listener (20 scenarios, 72 steps). Each step compares the status, every response header, the exact response BYTES and the exact `secrets.json` BYTES after the step: `/view` answers the active raw value per key; `/find` answers `{value}`, including an empty value for an unmatched id, and reproduces the `!state[key]` prototype-chain quirk where `constructor` and `toString` answer 200 while a file key outside `SECRET_KEYS` and the `_migrated` marker answer 404; `/delete` splices and reactivates the first remaining entry and removes the key when the array empties; `/rotate` deactivates every entry and activates the requested one; `/rename` appends an absent label and replaces a present one while storing a non-string label as submitted. The file semantics are pinned rather than assumed: reads CREATE a missing `secrets.json` (`{}` with no trailing newline) while `/delete` and `/rotate` do not and `/rename` does; a write deactivates the other entries for the key, defaults an ABSENT label to `Unlabeled` and re-serializes with four-space indentation and no trailing newline; a deliberate NON-write is discriminated from a rewrite by two-space-seeded files; a malformed file answers `{}` for `/read` and 500 elsewhere, and a null entry makes every projection throw. The routes are proven NOT to be behind `extensionsEnabledFeatureGuard`, because the pinned probe boots with `extensions.enabled: false` and still receives every answer. Known gap: a non-string truthy `key` is answered 400 rather than coerced through `ToPropertyKey`, which is unprobed and not claimed |

Each step compares the status, every response header, the exact response BYTES and the exact `secrets.json` BYTES after the step: `/view` answers the active raw value per key; `/find` answers `{value}`, including an empty value for an unmatched id, and reproduces the `!state[key]` prototype-chain quirk where `constructor` and `toString` answer 200 while a file key outside `SECRET_KEYS` and the `_migrated` marker answer 404; `/delete` splices and reactivates the first remaining entry and removes the key when the array empties; `/rotate` deactivates every entry and activates the requested one; `/rename` appends an absent label and replaces a present one while storing a non-string label as submitted. The file semantics are pinned rather than assumed: reads CREATE a missing `secrets.json` (`{}` with no trailing newline) while `/delete` and `/rotate` do not and `/rename` does; a write deactivates the other entries for the key, defaults an ABSENT label to `Unlabeled` and re-serializes with four-space indentation and no trailing newline; a deliberate NON-write is discriminated from a rewrite by two-space-seeded files; a malformed file answers `{}` for `/read` and 500 elsewhere, and a null entry makes every projection throw. The routes are proven NOT to be behind `extensionsEnabledFeatureGuard`, because the pinned probe boots with `extensions.enabled: false` and still receives every answer. Known gap: a non-string truthy `key` is answered 400 rather than coerced through `ToPropertyKey`, which is unprobed and not claimed |

| POST | `/api/speech/elevenlabs/history` | [src/endpoints/speech.js:274](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/speech.js#L274) | partial: nine of the eleven `/api/speech/*` routes are implemented and replayed against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-speech.json` (40 scenarios), recorded by `tools/probe-speech.mjs` from the real pinned server with only its `node-fetch` module replaced by a controlled peer (`tools/lib/pinned-outbound.mjs`) because every upstream here is a hardcoded literal with no seam. The Go side replays the same peer through a real local listener and produces the failure path through a CLOSED PORT; each base URL is a seam defaulting to the pinned URL and asserted. Compared per scenario: status, every header, the response BYTES (a SHA-256 digest for audio, the exact text for JSON), and the outbound method, URL, declared headers and body. Pinned behaviours preserved: a non-ok peer answers sendStatus(500); the Pollinations body has a fixed member order and a per-request random seed (normalized to a marker) and its audio is base64-decoded with the LENIENT Node decoder; an EMPTY `request` object is truthy so synthesize accepts it, and the sub-object is re-serialized (a body with `0.50` and `1e2` is normalized to `0.5` and `100`, which a raw pass-through would not do); the multipart part order is name, description, labels, files; a non-data-URI entry is skipped while a malformed-base64 data URI is still sent; `mime.extension` maps audio/mpeg to mpga; and recognize reads the upload from the global multer field `avatar` and forwards the file as audio.wav followed by model_id. Known gaps: the `/api/speech/recognize` and `/api/speech/synthesize` local-ONNX routes stay not implemented; the multer temp-file lifecycle in `_uploads` is not reproduced (Go parses the upload in memory and leaves no temp file), which the fixture records but deliberately does not compare; only the declared `xi-api-key` and `Authorization` headers are asserted |

| POST | `/api/translate/deepl` | [src/endpoints/translate.js:203](https://github.com/SillyTavern/SillyTavern/blob/06bde939fb1e9c4c8d8641d810f0a916b5bce127/src/endpoints/translate.js#L203) | partial: implemented and replayed against `tests/oracle/sillytavern-1.19.0-translate.json` (46 scenarios). Libre, DeepLX, OneRing and Lingva take their URL from a secret, so the Go test moves that secret origin onto a real local listener; DeepL and Yandex hardcode their endpoint and use seams defaulting to the pinned URL and asserted. Compared per scenario: status, every header, exact body BYTES, outbound method, path and query, and the exact outbound BODY bytes including parameter ORDER for both JSON and `application/x-www-form-urlencoded`. Pinned behaviours preserved: a `send(undefined)` answers 200 with no body, content type or content length; Lingva logs a non-ok answer and then parses it anyway; Libre always sends `api_key`, empty string included; DeepL maps `zh-CN` and `zh-TW` to `ZH` and adds `formality` only for its nine supported languages, with the body value `pro` selecting `api.deepl.com`; Yandex appends one `text` parameter per chunk in order and sends a fresh uuid-derived `ucid`; OneRing remaps `lang` but never sends it. The `url-join@5.0.0` normalization and `URLSearchParams.toString` are reproduced and mutation-tested. Known gaps: the DeepL `formality` value is the pinned default and a configured `deepl.formality` is not read; the Yandex `ucid` is nondeterministic and recorded as a marker; translated text is third-party data |

## Non-router HTTP surface

- `/`, `/callback/:source?`, `/login`, `/csrf-token`, `/version`, `/api/ping`, `/proxy/:url(*)`: registered in `src/server-main.js`.
- Public `GET /callback/:source?` redirect status, target, stable headers, and text/HTML bodies match pinned observations for encoded OAuth query values; the per-request timing header is normalized out and other route-level middleware remains outside that probe.
- `GET /login` matches the account-disabled redirect, pinned login page bytes/selected headers, and passwordless single-user auto-login session; proxy-auth integration and cache/compression behavior remain unimplemented.
- Embedded static serving matches the fresh-start oracle for `GET /` and `GET /lib.js` status, content type, and body digest; other static paths and middleware behavior remain unimplemented.
- The final 404 handler matches the seeded fresh-install page and a preexisting custom data-root `url-not-found.html` for GET; full middleware ordering remains unimplemented.
- `/csrf-token`, `/api/ping`, `/api/users/list`, and `/api/users/login` match pinned probes for account-enabled session identity, no-password auto-login, public user listing/login, Unicode-normalized scrypt, CSRF, signed 400-day cookies, token reuse, stale-session invalidation, and unknown-field preservation; recovery/management routes and remaining middleware still need probes.
- Exact authentication and the remaining route content-type, status, redirect, and error semantics require separate reference probes.

## Coverage boundaries

- This static inventory is not dynamic route coverage. Browser and extension traffic must still be captured.
- Arbitrary Node server-plugin routes are excluded by the project contract and are not enumerated as built-in SillyTavern APIs.
- The route count is a declaration count after resolving statically mounted routers; nested routers may contribute multiple full paths.
